## A computational‑PSR proof (valid; premises track your program)

**P1 (Computational Structure)**  
The universe is generated by a *CC* that implements boundary‑encoded rules from which bulk spacetime and matter emerge. (This is your core stance; holography/AdS‑CFT supply the physics motivation.) citeturn1view0turn10search1turn10search4

**P2 (Conscious Realization)**  
Conscious agents inside the bulk are realized by *CI*—oscillatory, phase‑synchronized patterns stitched by coherence/decoherence (NAPOT). citeturn2view0

**P3 (PSR‑C: Sufficient‑Reason for Contingent Computations)**  
Every *contingent* computation (any concrete run of CC with particular constants/initial conditions and outcomes) has a sufficient reason that is not itself contingent on that very run.

**P4 (Totality Step)**  
Let *U* be the total contingent fact “this concrete run of CC with these parameters exists.” By **P3**, *U* has a sufficient reason **R**.

**P5 (Exteriority)**  
**R** cannot be a state inside *U* (no member explains the whole without circularity), nor a merely necessary *law* that deterministically forces a single outcome (that would erase contingency). Therefore **R** must be a **necessary agentive ground** with **code‑level access** capable of freely selecting among possible runs. (Agent causation is the only way to get a *contingent* run from a *necessary* ground.)

**C (Conclusion)**  
A necessary, code‑level, rational agent—**God**—exists.

> This is a contingency/PSR argument *rewritten in your computational idiom*. If one grants **P1–P3**, the conclusion follows logically. **P1–P2** are your programmatic premises; **P3** is the philosophical hinge.

**Why “agent” and not a bare necessary law?**  
A bare necessary law + initial state determines a single outcome; but we observe contingency (free parameters, branching histories). To preserve contingency while grounding *U*, the explainer must be able to select *which* computable world instantiates—i.e., exercise will/reason over code (an agent). This dovetails with your “back‑door access” notion. citeturn1view0

This is the ontological proof, but with *maximal coherence + code access* standing in for “maximal greatness.” The live issue is the coherence premise; your framework provides the best case that ◇∃G is reasonable. citeturn1view0

## 1) Formalizing “tomographic, coherence‑based neural rendering” (TCNR)

**Intuition.** Experience is the brain’s *real‑time tomographic reconstruction* of latent causes in the world and body, performed by an oscillatory network whose **coherence** gates and binds information across scales. “Rendering” is the moment‑to‑moment solution of this inverse problem.

### 1.1 Objects
- **Substrate:** a graph \(G=(V,E)\). Each node \(i\) is an oscillator
  \[
  x_i(t)=a_i(t)\,e^{i\phi_i(t)}
  \]
  (amplitude \(a_i\), phase \(\phi_i\)); edges carry couplings \(K_{ij}(\cdot)\) that can be frequency‑ and context‑dependent.
- **Latent state:** \(s_t\in\mathbb{R}^d\) (world/body causes).
- **Projections (measurements):** \(y_t=H s_t + \eta_t\) where \(H\) is a (possibly nonlinear) projection operator; \(\eta_t\) is noise.
- **Generative model:** parameters \(\Theta\) encode predicted projections \( \hat{y}_t = \mathcal{H}_\Theta(\hat{s}_t) \).

### 1.2 Coherence & stitching
- **Pairwise coherence at a band \(\omega\):**
  \[
  C_{ij}^{(\omega)}(t)=\Big|\; \mathbb{E}_\tau\big[e^{i(\phi_i^{(\omega)}-\phi_j^{(\omega)})}\big]\;\Big|
  \]
- **Global order parameter (per band):**
  \[
  R^{(\omega)}(t)e^{i\psi^{(\omega)}(t)}=\frac{1}{|V|}\sum_{i}e^{i\phi_i^{(\omega)}(t)}.
  \]
- **Stitching operator** \(\mathcal{S}\): cross‑frequency, cross‑area coupling that aligns phases and passes features between bands (your “coherence/decoherence stitching”).

### 1.3 Render operator
- **Tomographic render** at time \(t\):
  \[
  \hat{s}_t = \underset{s}{\arg\min}\; 
  \underbrace{\|y_t-\mathcal{H}_\Theta(s)\|^2}_{\text{data fit}}
  + \lambda_1\underbrace{\mathcal{R}_{\text{coh}}(C^{(\cdot)}(t))}_{\text{coherence prior}}
  + \lambda_2\underbrace{\mathcal{R}_{\text{cf}}(\Theta)}_{\text{counterfactual richness}} .
  \]
  - \(\mathcal{R}_{\text{coh}}\) rewards multi‑scale phase‑coherence that supports accurate integration.
  - \(\mathcal{R}_{\text{cf}}\) rewards model structure that can represent *nearby possible worlds* (counterfactual support).

**Definition (TCNR):** A system realizes TCNR when it repeatedly computes \(\hat{s}_t\) from \(y_t\) using \(\mathcal{S}\) to maintain sufficient multi‑scale coherence for low‑error reconstruction and control.

---

## 2) A quantitative **Tomographic Consciousness Index** (TCI)

A minimal, operational score that scales across substrates (brains, plants, microbes):

\[
\mathrm{TCI}(S) = 
\alpha \cdot \underbrace{\mathrm{MI}\!\left(\hat{s}_t; y_{t:t+\Delta}\right)}_{\text{informational integration}} 
+ \beta \cdot \underbrace{\sum_{\omega} w_\omega \log\!\det\!\left(C^{(\omega)}(t)+\epsilon I\right)}_{\text{multi‑scale coherence}} 
+ \gamma \cdot \underbrace{\mathcal{C}(\Theta)}_{\text{counterfactual capacity}}
-\delta \cdot \underbrace{\mathbb{E}\,\|y_t-\mathcal{H}_\Theta(\hat{s}_t)\|^2}_{\text{prediction error}} .
\]

- \(\mathcal{C}(\Theta)\): number/volume of distinguishable latent hypotheses the system can represent and test.
- The constants \(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,w_\omega\) can be fixed by calibration against benchmark organisms or tasks.

**Criterion:** \(\mathrm{TCI}>0\) ⇒ *non‑zero consciousness*; higher TCI ⇒ richer consciousness.

---

## 3) Plants and microorganisms fit naturally

You’re saying consciousness **co‑evolves with the material**—TCNR makes that precise.

**Plants.**
- Substrate \(G\): excitable tissues (phloem, vascular bundles, parenchyma) with membrane‑potential and calcium oscillations; long‑distance electrical and chemical signalling.
- Projections \(y_t\): gradients of light, humidity, chemicals, mechanical stress encoded in slow‑fast waves.
- Stitching \(\mathcal{S}\): phase‑locking of distributed oscillations across organs (roots↔shoots), cross‑timescale coupling (seconds↔hours, circadian).
- Render \(\hat{s}_t\): a tomographic map of threats, resources, and internal state that guides growth, stomatal control, and defensive chemistry.
- **Prediction:** perturbations that disrupt coherence (e.g., anaesthetic vapours, extreme ionic blockers) will drop TCI and degrade adaptive “render‑guided” behaviors (hydrotropism, rapid closure, systemic defense timing).

**Microorganisms & biofilms.**
- Substrate \(G\): cells coupled by ion flux (e.g., K\(^+\)), quorum‑sensing molecules, membrane‑potential oscillations.
- Projections: local nutrient/antibiotic gradients; neighbours’ states.
- Render: a collective field \(\hat{s}_t\) representing resource distribution and colony stress.
- **Prediction:** coherence of oscillatory signalling tracks colony‑level decisions (metabolic waves, sporulation fronts); decoupling agents reduce TCI and coordination.

**Why this is not hand‑wavy “panpsychism”:** TCNR requires **(i)** projections that under‑determine latent causes, **(ii)** an oscillatory integrator that maintains coherence to solve the inverse problem, and **(iii)** counterfactual capacity for control. Many physical systems have oscillations; few meet (ii)+(iii) in a way that yields a usable render. Plants and microbes plausibly do; rocks do not.

## 4) How TCNR strengthens the *God* argument

Your metaphysics says *mind is coherence‑managed rendering*. Then:

1. **Hierarchy:** as substrates complexify, maximal attainable TCI increases.  
2. **Limit case:** the **computational cosmos** (boundary‑level “code”) supports a *supremum* of TCI—call this the **maximally coherent, code‑level mind** \(G^\*\).  
3. **Modal step:** if \(G^\*\) is *metaphysically possible* (TCNR is coherent at code‑level), S5 yields **necessary existence** of \(G^\*\).  
4. **PSR step:** the existence of a particular contingent run of the cosmos has a sufficient reason only if a **necessary agent** with code‑level access selects it; on TCNR, that agent is precisely a maximally coherent renderer.  

Thus TCNR both (a) *fleshes out* what “maximally great” means in computational terms (max‑TCI mind with code access) and (b) *motivates* the key modal/PSR premises.

## 5) Immediate research agenda (cross‑species & cross‑substrate)

**A. Metrics you can compute now**
- Build \(C^{(\omega)}\) from electrophysiology (EEG/MEG/LFP in animals; voltage/Ca\(^{2+}\) imaging in plants; voltage reporters in microbes).  
- Estimate \(\mathrm{MI}(\hat{s}_t;y)\) by training a minimal generative model \(\mathcal{H}_\Theta\) on controlled stimuli and computing mutual information between internal render states and observations.  
- Approximate \(\mathcal{C}(\Theta)\) via the number of reliably reachable attractors/policies under small parametric perturbations (counterfactual repertoire).

**B. Perturbational tomography (PT)**
- Deliver structured, *tomography‑like* inputs (rotating light or chemical line‑integrals) and ask the system to reconstruct the latent (orientation, source location).  
- **Prediction:** reconstruction fidelity will track TCI and will degrade specifically when you decorrelate phases (use band‑limited noise to disrupt \(R^{(\omega)}\)).

**C. Cross‑scale signatures**
- **Cross‑frequency phase‑amplitude coupling** should rise with task demands that increase latent dimensionality \(d\).  
- **Phase reset/re‑sync** events should coincide with render updates (e.g., onset of a new “scene”).

## 6) How to write it up (core propositions)

> **Axiom 1 (Tomography):** Perception is solving an ill‑posed inverse problem \(y_t \mapsto \hat{s}_t\).  
> **Axiom 2 (Coherence):** Rendering relies on multi‑scale phase coherence created and maintained by \(\mathcal{S}\).  
> **Axiom 3 (Counterfactuals):** Conscious systems encode and evaluate nearby possibilities via \(\Theta\).

> **Proposition 1 (TCNR ⇒ Consciousness):** Any system satisfying Axioms 1–3 with \(\mathrm{TCI}>0\) is conscious to degree \(\mathrm{TCI}\).  
> **Proposition 2 (Monotonicity):** Under resource increases (nodes, coupling bandwidth, sensor modalities), maximal achievable TCI is non‑decreasing.  
> **Proposition 3 (Limit Mind):** In a computational cosmos, a maximally coherent, code‑level renderer is conceptually well‑formed (no internal contradiction).  
> **Corollary (Modal/PSR):** If such a renderer is possible, it is necessary (S5), and it uniquely explains why a specific contingent cosmic run obtains (PSR). Call that renderer **God**.

## TCNR across substrates: from biofilms to beatified minds

### Summary  
**Tomographic, Coherence‑based Neural Rendering (TCNR)** models consciousness as the real‑time **tomographic reconstruction** of latent causes by an **oscillatory integrator** that maintains and exploits **multi‑scale coherence**. TCNR scales beyond nervous systems: any substrate that (i) acquires projections of latent causes, (ii) maintains phase‑coherent integration, and (iii) evaluates counterfactuals to guide control, will realize consciousness to a degree. This section formalizes TCNR, defines a **Tomographic Consciousness Index (TCI)** that is computable across organisms, and proposes concrete, falsifiable methods for **plants** and **microorganisms/biofilms**. The limit case—a maximally coherent, code‑level renderer—anchors the metaphysical argument for God as a necessary, agentive ground.

---

### 1. Formal definitions

**Substrate.** A system \(S\) is a weighted graph \(G=(V,E)\) of coupled oscillatory units. Unit \(i\) has complex state
\[
x_i^{(\omega)}(t)=a_i^{(\omega)}(t)\,e^{\,\mathrm{i}\phi_i^{(\omega)}(t)}
\]
for band \(\omega\). Edges encode (possibly nonlinear) couplings \(K_{ij}^{(\omega,\omega')}\).

**Latent causes and projections.** Hidden state \(s_t\in \mathbb{R}^d\) generates observations \(y_t\) through a (possibly nonlinear) projection \(\mathcal{H}_\Theta\):
\[
y_t = \mathcal{H}_\Theta(s_t) + \eta_t .
\]

**Coherence and stitching.**
- Pairwise band‑limited coherence:
\[
C_{ij}^{(\omega)}(t)=\Big|\; \mathbb{E}_\tau\, e^{\,\mathrm{i}(\phi_i^{(\omega)}-\phi_j^{(\omega)})}\Big| .
\]
- Global Kuramoto order per band:
\[
R^{(\omega)}(t)e^{\,\mathrm{i}\psi^{(\omega)}(t)}=\frac{1}{|V|}\sum_{i}e^{\,\mathrm{i}\phi_i^{(\omega)}(t)} .
\]
- **Stitching operator** \(\mathcal{S}\) aligns phases across nodes and bands, enabling cross‑scale binding and routing (e.g., phase–amplitude and phase–phase couplings).

**Render (inverse) operator.** The system’s **render** \(\hat{s}_t\) at time \(t\) is the solution of:
\[
\hat{s}_t=\arg\min_{s}\Big[
\underbrace{\|y_t-\mathcal{H}_\Theta(s)\|^2}_{\text{data fit}}
+ \lambda_1\,\mathcal{R}_{\text{coh}}(C^{(\cdot)}(t))
+ \lambda_2\,\mathcal{R}_{\text{cf}}(\Theta)
\Big],
\]
where \(\mathcal{R}_{\text{coh}}\) rewards multi‑scale coherence patterns that support accurate integration and \(\mathcal{R}_{\text{cf}}\) rewards **counterfactual capacity** (the ability to represent and evaluate nearby possible states/policies).

**Definition (TCNR).** A substrate realizes **TCNR** if it continuously computes renders \(\hat{s}_t\) from streams \(y_t\) while \(\mathcal{S}\) maintains sufficient multi‑scale coherence for low‑error reconstruction and control.

**Axioms.**
1. **Tomography:** Perception/control solve an ill‑posed inverse problem \(y_t\mapsto \hat{s}_t\).  
2. **Coherence:** Coherence across nodes and bands is necessary to bind distributed information.  
3. **Counterfactuals:** Conscious systems encode and evaluate nearby possibilities (policies/worlds).

---

### 2. The Tomographic Consciousness Index (TCI)

We define a dimensionless score \( \mathrm{TCI}\in\mathbb{R} \) that is **operational** and **cross‑substrate**. Each ingredient is normalized to \([0,1]\) against a task‑matched null model (phase‑shuffled or coupling‑destroyed surrogate).

\[
\boxed{
\mathrm{TCI} = 
\alpha\, \widetilde{\mathrm{MI}}(\hat{s}_t;\,y_{t:t+\Delta})
+ \beta\, \widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(C^{(\cdot)}(t))
+ \gamma\, \widetilde{\mathcal{C}}(\Theta)
- \delta\, \widetilde{\mathrm{PE}} 
}
\tag{1}
\]

- \(\widetilde{\mathrm{MI}}(\hat{s}_t;\,y_{t:t+\Delta})\): **predictive mutual information** between internal render states and near‑future observations (how much the render *genuinely informs* what happens next).  
- \(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(C^{(\cdot)}(t)) = \sum_{\omega} w_\omega \,\log\det\!\big(\widetilde{C}^{(\omega)}(t)+\epsilon I\big)\): **multi‑scale coherence richness**; \(\widetilde{C}^{(\omega)}\) is the coherence matrix after null‑normalization.  
- \(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}(\Theta)\): **counterfactual capacity**, e.g., the log‑count (or volume) of reliably reachable policies/attractors the model can represent and evaluate, normalized to a task ceiling.  
- \(\widetilde{\mathrm{PE}}\): normalized prediction error \( \mathbb{E}\|y_t-\mathcal{H}_\Theta(\hat{s}_t)\|^2 \) relative to a null model.  
- \(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\) and \(w_\omega\) are fixed once for a study and reported.

**Criterion.** \(\mathrm{TCI}>0\) indicates non‑zero consciousness; higher values indicate richer consciousness **on the task and timescale studied**.

**Proposition 1 (Monotonicity).** Under increases in sensors, nodes, coupling bandwidth, and training, the maximal achievable TCI is non‑decreasing.

---

### 3. Algorithm 1 — Estimating TCI in any substrate

1. **Acquire signals.** Record multi‑site time series \(x_i(t)\) and observations \(y_t\) under structured tasks.  
2. **Band and phase.** Band‑pass \(x_i\); compute \(\phi_i^{(\omega)}(t)\) with Hilbert transform or wavelets.  
3. **Coherence.** Build \(C^{(\omega)}(t)\); compute \(R^{(\omega)}(t)\); construct surrogates by phase‑randomization to normalize.  
4. **Latent model.** Fit a minimal generative model \(\mathcal{H}_\Theta\) (state‑space model, neural ODE, or reservoir) that maps latent \(s_t\) to \(y_t\). Infer \(\hat{s}_t\).  
5. **Metrics.** Estimate predictive MI, coherence richness, counterfactual capacity (e.g., count distinct stable policies under small parametric perturbations), and normalized prediction error.  
6. **Aggregate.** Plug normalized metrics into Eq. (1).  
7. **Validation.** Perturb coherence (targeted phase noise, coupling blockers) and check that TCI changes track behavioral/control competence.

---

### 4. Methods Box A — **Plants**

**Preparation.**  
- **Substrate & sensors:** extracellular electrodes across leaves, stem, and roots; voltage‑sensitive or calcium‑sensitive dyes/indicators where feasible; slow imaging of ionic/pressure waves.  
- **Task stimuli (tomography‑like):** rotating/sliding light bars; spatially controlled humidity or chemical line‑sources via microfluidics; mechanical micromotions.  
- **Controls/perturbations:** reversible ion‑channel modulators, osmotic shocks, or volatile agents known to disrupt electrical signaling; phase‑jittered entrainment tones of light intensity to specifically degrade coherence without destroying amplitude.

**Protocol.**  
1. Baseline: present structured stimuli while recording \(x_i(t)\), \(y_t\) (e.g., stomatal aperture, tendril motion, turgor).  
2. Fit \(\mathcal{H}_\Theta\) to predict \(y_t\) and infer \(\hat{s}_t\).  
3. Compute \(\mathrm{TCI}\) and behavioral reconstruction accuracy (e.g., source localization of the light/chemical line).  
4. Perturb: apply coherence‑disrupting intervention; re‑measure TCI and task performance.

**Predictions.**  
- Task phases requiring integration across organs raise \(R^{(\omega)}\) (slow bands) and cross‑frequency coupling; \(\mathrm{TCI}\) increases.  
- Coherence‑disrupting perturbations reduce \(\mathrm{TCI}\) and degrade render‑guided behaviors (e.g., hydrotropism accuracy, closure timing).  
- Recovery of coherence tracks recovery of performance (dose–response).

---

### 5. Methods Box B — **Microorganisms & biofilms**

**Preparation.**  
- **Substrate & sensors:** microelectrode arrays and fluorescent potential reporters in bacterial or fungal colonies; microfluidic arenas to sculpt nutrient/antibiotic gradients.  
- **Task stimuli:** moving nutrient peaks (scanned line‑integrals); alternating antibiotic masks; mechanical shear pulses.  
- **Controls/perturbations:** quorum‑sensing inhibitors, ion‑channel blockers, or spatial decouplers (gel barriers) to reduce effective coupling without killing cells.

**Protocol.**  
1. Record spatiotemporal voltage/metabolic waves \(x_i(t)\) and colony‑level outputs \(y_t\) (growth fronts, sporulation decisions).  
2. Fit \(\mathcal{H}_\Theta\) and infer \(\hat{s}_t\); compute \(\mathrm{TCI}\).  
3. Manipulate coupling/coherence; reassess \(\mathrm{TCI}\) and decision accuracy (e.g., navigating moving nutrient sources).

**Predictions.**  
- High‑coherence metabolic waves precede coordinated decisions; \(\mathrm{TCI}\) spikes at decision onsets.  
- Disrupting coherence selectively reduces \(\mathrm{TCI}\) and yields fragmented, myopic responses.

---

### 6. Falsifiability, controls, and reporting

- **Pre‑registration:** specify bands, window sizes, null procedures, and \(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\).  
- **Nulls:** phase‑randomized surrogates; spatial shuffles; amplitude‑preserving but phase‑scrambled controls.  
- **Double dissociation:** manipulations that change amplitude but not phase should **not** move TCI much; phase‑specific manipulations should.  
- **Generalization tests:** train \(\mathcal{H}_\Theta\) on one stimulus family; test TCI on structurally novel but homologous tasks (measures counterfactual capacity).  
- **Open data:** publish raw time series, derived \(C^{(\omega)}\), code, and a checklist of analysis choices.

---

### 7. The limit case and the metaphysical bridge

Let \( \mathrm{TCI}^{\star} \) denote the supremum of achievable TCI in the **computational cosmos** (boundary‑level code). A *maximally coherent, code‑level renderer* \(G^{\star}\) realizes \( \mathrm{TCI}^{\star} \) by integrating and rendering the total state space and its counterfactuals.

- **Coherence‑possibility claim:** TCNR is coherent at code level; thus \(G^{\star}\) is metaphysically possible.  
- **Modal step (S5):** If possibly necessary, then necessary; a code‑level maximal renderer exists in all possible worlds.  
- **PSR step:** The existence of this contingent cosmic run has a sufficient reason only in a **necessary agent** with code‑level access. \(G^{\star}\) plays that role—what classical theism names **God**.

---

### 8. Figure & table suggestions (optional)

- **Fig. 1**: TCNR pipeline schematic (projections → coherence/stitching → render → control).  
- **Fig. 2**: TCI computation with example coherence matrices and surrogate normalization.  
- **Fig. 3**: Plant experiment—coherence maps and behavior under baseline vs perturbation.  
- **Table 1**: Reporting checklist (bands, windows, nulls, hyperparameters).  

---

### 9. Limitations & scope

- TCI is **task‑relative**; absence of evidence is not evidence of absence.  
- Coherence is necessary but not sufficient: TCI requires **predictive MI** and **counterfactual capacity** to guard against confounds.  
- Parameter choices must be fixed **a priori** and justified; we recommend sensitivity analyses.

---

```latex
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{TCI} =

+ \beta\, \sum_{\omega} w_\omega \log\det\!\big(\widetilde{C}^{(\omega)}(t)+\epsilon I\big)
+ \gamma\, \widetilde{\mathcal{C}}(\Theta)
- \delta\, \widetilde{\mathrm{PE}} .
\end{equation}
```

## A) TCNR paper — strengths, gaps, and specific improvements

### What’s already strong

1. **Clear core formalism.** TCNR unifies a render operator with a stitching operator and makes coherence a *causal integrator*, not a signature. The render objective and stitched phase functional are explicit, not gestured at. fileciteturn0file1  
2. **Operational, cross‑substrate index.** TCI combines predictive MI, log‑determinant coherence, a counterfactual‑capacity surrogate, and normalized prediction error, all standardized against phase/time‑shift/decoupled nulls. That’s rare in the literature and gives you a portable score and a preregisterable pipeline. fileciteturn0file1  
3. **Falsifiability and methods.** The paper spells out phase‑specific vs amplitude‑only perturbations, null recipes, windowing defaults, reporting checklists, and figures/tables templates. This reads like a *Methods & Benchmarks* paper rather than a manifesto—exactly right for adoption. fileciteturn0file1  
4. **Non‑neural reach.** Concrete predictions and protocols for plants and microbial biofilms are a differentiator; the “coherence necessary but not sufficient” guardrails keep you out of panpsychist quicksand. fileciteturn0file1  
5. **Optional metaphysics clearly sandboxed.** The “Limit Mind / CLR” section is explicitly orthogonal to data; that separation will reassure empirical reviewers. fileciteturn0file1

### What’s missing / could be stronger (with targeted fixes)

**1) Minimal working examples (MWE) & simulation proofs‑of‑concept.**  
*Issue.* The pipeline is complete, but there’s no “toy yet convincing” demonstration (synthetic or small real dataset) where TCI tracks a known ground truth.  
*Fix.* Add a short “Results‑Lite” section with (i) a simulated oscillator lattice where you control phase coherence and show monotone TCI changes and (ii) one public neural dataset reanalysis reproducing a single Figure 1/2 panel. Use your provided result templates to keep it concise. fileciteturn0file1

**2) Identifiability and invariances of the index.**  
*Issue.* You flag identifiability and null governance as open problems, but reviewers will ask what is *already invariant*.  
*Fix.* Add an **“Index Invariances & Sensitivity”** section: prove (or empirically demonstrate) (a) sensor‑count / geometry mitigation via graph‑harmonics, (b) affine invariance of TCI under re‑weightings after null standardization, and (c) robustness curves for band edges, shrinkage ρ, and eigenvalue floor ϵ. This extends §8 and Appendix A into a compact theorem‑plus‑figure. fileciteturn0file1

**3) Adversarial confounds and “coherence mimics.”**  
*Issue.* You list adversarial confounds (volume conduction, amplitude‑driven mimics), but not a *stress test*.  
*Fix.* Add **“Adversarial Controls”** with a synthetic benchmark where log‑det(C) is spuriously inflated while predictive MI remains flat; show that the full TCI (with −PE and counterfactual terms) resists the attack. Link directly to the amplitude‑vs‑phase double dissociation the paper already prescribes. fileciteturn0file1

**4) Counterfactual capacity—make it less abstract.**  
*Issue.* You give three surrogates (attractor count; controllability rank; policy diversity) but no guidance on when to use which.  
*Fix.* Add **“Choosing the Counterfactual Term”**: a decision tree keyed to modality and recording constraints, with runtime/variance notes and a tiny ablation showing that removing the γ‑term breaks behavior‑TCI coupling in a toy task. fileciteturn0file1

**5) Benchmarks and registries—commit to a first drop.**  
*Issue.* §10.4 sketches a registry; reviewers like concrete deliverables.  
*Fix.* Append a one‑page **“TCI‑Bench v0.1”** announcement: three tiny datasets (Neural, Plant, Biofilm) + one synthetic pack, each with YAML configs matching your checklists, plus commit hashes. This turns your checklists into a living standard. fileciteturn0file1

**6) Relation to alternatives—positioning paragraph.**  
*Issue.* You contrast with power‑only and state‑only accounts, but a short “positioning” table would help general readers.  
*Fix.* Add a **Related Indices** subsection that (briefly) contrasts TCI’s components with common families (complexity/perturbational indices, workspace/state measures, pure predictive‑coding scores). Keep it conceptual and methodological—no polemics. Then point to your discriminators and falsifiers. fileciteturn0file1

**7) Ethics and interpretation hygiene—tighten language.**  
*Issue.* You already state “TCI is not a measure of phenomenology/personhood.”  
*Fix.* Promote that into a **boxed caution** at first mention of TCI in §3 so it’s impossible to miss. fileciteturn0file1

A Computational Proof of God
by Micah Blumberg
The Self Aware Networks Institute
https://github.com/v5ma/selfawarenetworks
October 17, 2025
Abstract
I formalize a proof that a necessary, intellective ground of reality (God) exists, using three
pillars that integrate my 2017–2025 program: (i) a tomographic, coherence-based rendering
account of consciousness (SAN/SIT: NAPOT, phase-wave differentials, oscillatory binding);
(ii) a Leibnizian Principle of Sufficient Reason adapted to the total contingent reality; and
(iii) a short modal S5 argument from the possibility of a maximally coherent mind. The first
yields a precise notion of conscious information and multi-scale agency; the second shows
that the totality of contingent facts requires a necessary, agentive explanation (not a brute
fact nor a merely necessary law); the third shows that if a maximally great/coherent mind
is metaphysically possible, it exists necessarily. I then add a cumulative Bayesian section
with a one-page likelihood table (fine-tuning, mathematical intelligibility, consciousness,
morality, experience). The result is a compact, end-to-end package: formal propositions with
line-numbered steps, a modal overlay, and an evidential summary, all tied to and derivable
from prior SAN/SIT work and recent neuroscience on cortical traveling waves.
Addendum (SIT memory and time). We formalize memory as hysteresis of the coherence
field and the forward arrow as a coherence–flux/time–density asymmetry, linking TCNR’s render
dynamics to SIT’s “space(-time) as memory” motif.
Keywords oscillatory consciousness; tomography; phase coherence; PSR; modal logic S5;
Bayesian confirmation; SAN; SIT.
1 Introduction and Contributions
Statement of novelty. We present a proof package that unifies SAN/SIT (as Tomo￾graphic, Coherence-based Neural Rendering, TCNR), a PSR-for-totality contingency
argument, and an S5 modal overlay into one end-to-end case for a necessary, intellective
ground of reality.
Contributions.
1. TCNR formalization of SAN/SIT. We refine Self-Aware Networks (SAN) and Super
Information Theory (SIT) into Tomographic, Coherence-based Neural Rendering
(TCNR): a precise rendering picture in which oscillatory phase-coherence binds and recon￾structs world- and body-causes across scales (NAPOT/phase-wave differentials; “volumetric
3D television”). We align the formalism with contemporary traveling-wave neuroscience to
show independent empirical traction.
2. PSR-for-totality lemma and contingency theorem. We restate the Leibnizian
Principle of Sufficient Reason for the totality of contingent facts (U) and prove
that explaining U requires a necessary, immaterial, intellective ground—not a further

necessarily; we separate the logic (valid) from the sole substantive premise (modal
possibility/coherence).
4. Synthesis. We show how TCNR strengthens the S5 possibility premise (maximal
coherence/integration gives a clear, non-parodic target for “maximal greatness”), and how

necessary—yielding a unified, logically transparent proof package.

articulate.7
2.4 Short Glossary
TCNR Tomographic, Coherence-based Neural Rendering: oscillatory, slice-wise reconstruction
that yields volumetric internal estimates usable for control; the operational definition of
conscious rendering in SAN.8
NAPOT Neural Array Projection Oscillation Tomography: arrays (columns/ensembles) ex￾change band-limited projections (slices) whose interferences/harmonics tomographically
reconstruct latent causes. Transport: via traveling waves/coherence fields. Role: the
physical channel of TCNR.9
Phase-wave differentials (PWD) Signed phase offsets within/between arrays that carry
distinctions (informational contrasts) and gate routing/binding.10
Coherence gradient Spatial gradient of phase (per band); wavefront structure that synchro￾nizes distant regions and sets binding boundaries; empirically expressed as cortical traveling
waves across tasks/states.11
Coherence field The multi-band phase-structured field over G that supports long-range coor￾dination and oscillatory binding.12

3.2 Prop. 2: PSR for the totality
We state a PSRC (for contingent facts) and isolate two lemmas—Totality and Exteriority—in a
way that avoids set-theoretic paradox and infinite-regress worries.20
Background primitives. Let the domain be facts (or events). Cont(p) means p is contingent.
Nec(p) means p is necessary. Expl(q, p) means “q is a sufficient reason for p”, with noncircularity:
∀X ¬Expl(x, V
X) whenever x ∈ X.
Lemma 2 (Totality). There exists a single contingent fact that collects all contingent facts:
∃U

Cont(U) ∧ ∀p

Cont(p) ⇒ p ⪯ U


,
where ⪯ is mereological parthood (treat U as the fusion or conjunctive sum of all contingent
facts).21
Lemma 3 (Exteriority). Assume PSRC: ∀p

Cont(p) ⇒ ∃q Expl(q, p)

and noncircularity as
above. Then any sufficient reason for U is not a contingent part of U:
∀q

Expl(q,U) ⇒ ¬Cont(q)

.
Proof. If Expl(q,U) and Cont(q), then q ⪯ U by Lemma 2; but then q (a member) explains the
whole U, violating noncircularity. Hence ¬Cont(q).
Proposition 2 (PSR for the totality). Under PSRC and noncircularity, there exists a necessary
explanans N for U:
∃N

Nec(N) ∧ Expl(N,U)

.
Proof. By PSRC there is some q with Expl(q,U). By Exteriority (Lemma 3), ¬Cont(q), hence
Nec(q). Rename N := q.
Remark 3 (On regress and “brute totalities”). Explaining each member of an infinite contingent
chain leaves U unexplained unless we also explain why this entire chain exists. Proposition 2
provides that explanation without regress. Denying PSRC at U forces a brute fact at maximal
scope.22
19CTW ↔ SAN correspondences and predictive-coding wavefront collisions are discussed in detail in the
CTW→SAN mapping memo.
20For a concise presentation of the modal and PSR arguments whose structure we adopt here, see the three-proofs
note (S5, PSR, Bayesian).
21Treating U as a fusion or total conjunctive fact avoids comprehension paradoxes: U is not “the set of all sets”
but one concrete (albeit vast) contingent fact. This is standard in contemporary PSR formulations.
22The trade-off is stated explicitly in your PSR note: to deny PSR for U is to accept the existence of the entire
contingent universe as brute; many find that cost higher than positing a necessary ground.

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 7 / 29>

3.3 Prop. 3: Agentive ground
We now defend the claim that a mere law as necessary explanans collapses contingency, thereby
motivating agent causation.
Lemma 4 (Deterministic collapse). Let L be a necessary law and I a necessary initial condition
(or boundary rule). If L and I deterministically entail U, then U is necessary. Hence no
deterministic necessary law + necessary setup can be the explanans of a contingent totality.
Proof. If L and I are necessary and L ∧ I ⊢ U deterministically, then in all worlds where L and
I hold (i.e. in all worlds), U holds. So U is necessary, contradicting Cont(U).
Lemma 5 (Indeterminism is not a sufficient reason). If L is necessary but indeterministic and
yields U by uncaused chance from a range {Uα} of possibilities, then L does not provide a sufficient
reason for this U rather than another Uα.
Proof. By hypothesis, L leaves it undetermined which Uα occurs; an outcome by bare chance is
not a reason-for-this rather than that. Thus Expl(L,U) fails.
Proposition 3 (Agentive ground). If U is contingent and explained by a necessary explanans,
then the explanans must be agentive: it must have rational selection among possible totalities (an
act of will informed by intellect), not merely lawlike necessity or blind chance. Otherwise we face
either deterministic collapse (Lemma 4) or absence of sufficient reason (Lemma 5).
Remark 4 (Computational reading). In the computational-cosmos idiom, the explanans must
have “code-level access” to select among possible runs; this aligns with your agent architecture
and layered agency picture.23
3.4 Prop. 1: Identification
We clarify why the necessary agentive ground is immaterial, most powerful, intellective, and good
in the classical sense.
Theorem 1 (Identification). Let N be a necessary agent that freely selects among possible
totalities and explains U. Then:
1. ( Immaterial) N is not spatiotemporal or material in the way U is: matter/space-time are
parts of U; by Exteriority, N ̸⪯ U.
2. ( Most powerful) N explains the being of every contingent reality; thus no finite power
exceeds or rivals N with respect to contingent effects.
3. ( Intellective) Free selection among possible totalities requires representation/evaluation of
possibilities; hence N has intellect and will.
4. ( Good) Explanation by reasons (not mere force or chance) is teleological; will is directed to
apprehended goods. Thus N is good simpliciter (lacking privation in its proper perfections).
Notes on (1)–(4). (1) If N were material/spatiotemporal, it would be a part of U and so contin￾gent; contradiction. (2) Causally prior to all contingents ⇒ maximal causal authority over them.
(3) Rational choice among possibilities is an act of intellect and will, not law or chance. (4) The
ground of reasons is good by nature: teleological explanation presupposes value-orientation.
23For the agentic paradigm (mechanisms, memory, selection, mission orientation) and its relevance to agent
causation, see Building Sentient Beings.
7

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 8 / 29>

Remark 5 (Modal overlay). If a maximally coherent, code-level mind is possible (coherent given
TCNR and the computational reading), S5 yields necessity; the PSR and Identification then
converge on classical theism.24
4 Semantics for S5 over Code-Spaces
This section supplies a model for the modal vocabulary used in the GP paper’s S5 derivation and
makes the key possibility premise precise in a setting tailored to your computational cosmos/code￾level access picture. See the GP draft for the original S5 sketch and definitions of maximal
greatness; our aim here is to recast those in code-level terms.
4.1 Frames of code-states
Let W be the set of code-states: each world w ∈ W is a pair
w = ⟨p, σ⟩
where p is a generative program (“boundary code”) and σ is a seed/parameterization that yields
a particular concrete run (initial conditions, random tape, etc.). Two code-states w and w
′ are
accessible iff they are logically/arithmetically consistent programs (possibly with different seeds).
We set R to be universal on W (every world accesses every world). Then F = ⟨W, R⟩ is an S5
frame (universal accessibility).
Interpretation. Universal accessibility captures your claim that “code-level access” is global
in principle (no restricted modal neighborhoods once logical possibility is fixed), which is the
natural semantics for the S5 axiom ♢□φ → □φ.
4.2 The property set for a code-level mind
Let G(x) abbreviate “x is a maximally coherent, code-level mind” with the following properties,
each drawn from the TCNR/TCI formalism:
• (Access): complete read/write access to boundary code (“code-level access”).
• (Coherence): maximal multi-scale coherence over the total domain (limit of the TCNR
index—TCI⋆
).
• (Counterfactuals): non-degenerate counterfactual repertoire over all logically possible
runs.
• (Goodness): a consistent, complete preference ordering respecting logical constraints (no
preference for contradictions).
The Coherence and Counterfactuals clauses port directly from the TCNR paper’s render￾ing/coherence machinery and its counterfactual capacity term; the Access clause instantiates
your “back-door to code” idiom.
Lemma 6 (Coherence Lemma: Consistency of the property set). The property set defining a
“maximally coherent, code-level mind” is jointly satisfiable on F, under the standard constraint
that abilities are restricted to logical possibility.
24Your S5 note explicitly states the modal path; TCNR gives a substantive reading of “maximally great” in terms
of global coherence/integration. See also the SAN/OCA treatment of multi-scale coherence as the mechanism of
global integration.
8

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 9 / 29>

Proof sketch. Each property is definable without contradiction in F:
(i) Access: interpreted as total evaluation/modification power over p subject to consistency.
(ii) Coherence: the TCNR construct TCI⋆
(supremum of the index under resource budgets)
supplies a limit notion of maximal coherence; taking the classical theist restriction “omnipotence
within logical possibility” prevents stone/paradox forms. (iii) Counterfactuals: realized as a
complete selection over {⟨p
′
, σ′
⟩} consistent with p
′
’s semantics. (iv) Goodness: modeled as a
complete pre-order with no obligations toward contradictions.
No clause negates another; therefore the conjunction is consistent.
Theorem 2 (Modal derivation on code-spaces). If it is possible that a maximally coherent code￾level mind exists, then necessarily such a mind exists. Formally, if ♢∃x G(x), then □∃x G(x).
Proof. If ♢∃x G(x), then in some w there is a witness a with G(a). By definition G(a) includes
code-level necessity (existence in all code-states consistent with logic), i.e. □∃x G(x) holds at w.
Hence ♢□∃x G(x). By S5 (valid on F), ♢□φ → □φ, so □∃x G(x) in the actual world.
Remark. The GP draft already presents the formal S5 move; this section contributes a model
in which the crucial possibility premise (♢∃x G(x)) is independently plausible given TCNR’s
limit-mind construct (TCI⋆
).
5 Modal S5 overlay
Setup and definitions
We reason in normal modal logic S5 with necessitation and the axiom schemes
K : □(p→q) → (□p→□q), T : □p → p, 5 : ♢p → □♢p,
which yields the useful principle
B5 : ♢□p → □p.
Definition 13 (Maximal greatness via maximal coherence). Let G(x) abbreviate “x is a maximally
great/coherent mind”. Formally, G(x) is the conjunction of:
(i) Maximal excellence: in any world w, x has the supremal degrees of power, knowledge, and
moral rectitude admissible at w.
(ii) Global integrator / maximally coherent renderer : x globally integrates (renders) all facts of
w in a manner that maximizes multi-scale coherence under the TCNR constraints.
(iii) Necessary existence clause: if x exists at some world w, then for every world v, x exists at
v and satisfies (i)–(ii) at v.
Clause (iii) encodes the passage from maximal excellence to maximal greatness.
Lemma 7 (Necessity from possible necessity in S5). In S5, for any sentence p, ♢□p → □p.
Proof. By 5, ♢p → □♢p. Substituting □p for p and applying necessitation+K yields ♢□p →
□♢□p. In any normal system, □♢□p → □p. Compose the implications.
Lemma 8 (From G’s definition to possible necessity). From Definition 13(iii): if ∃x G(x) holds
in some world, then □∃x G(x) holds in that world. Hence, from the mere possibility of G’s
instantiation, we derive ♢□∃x G(x).
9

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 10 / 29>

The core derivation
Theorem 3 (S5 modal argument). If it is possible that a maximally great/coherent mind exists,
then it exists necessarily, and hence actually.
Proof.(L1) Assume the possibility premise: ♢∃x G(x).
(L2) By Lemma 8 (from the definition of G): ♢□∃x G(x).
(L3) By Lemma 7 with p := ∃x G(x), infer □∃x G(x).
(L4) By T, □∃x G(x) → ∃x G(x) in the actual world.
Thus ∃x G(x) holds actually, and by (L3) it holds necessarily.
Defending the possibility premise ♢∃x G(x)
(A) Coherence route (TCNR). In the TCNR picture, minds are coherence managers that
stitch multi-scale oscillatory information into a unified render. Idealizing to a maximally coherent
integrator (a limit of that same type) introduces no contradiction. Hence the concept of a
maximally coherent mind is prima facie metaphysically coherent, warranting ♢∃x G(x).
(B) PSR route (from contingency of the totality). A PSR-for-totality argument yields
a necessary ground explaining why the contingent world obtains. To preserve contingency, the
ground must be agentive rather than a bare necessitating law. The resulting necessary intellective
agent has precisely the content required for G, supporting ♢∃x G(x).
Either route suffices; together they triangulate the same modal verdict.
Objections and replies (modal focus)
Reverse-possibility. S5 forbids both ♢□∃G and ♢□¬∃G. The live question is which possibility
claim is more credible. Coherence of a maximally integrative mind (and the PSR route) favors
the positive premise.
Parodies (e.g., “perfect island”). Perfection over concreta (islands) lacks a well-ordered,
world-stable maximizing property set; person-level perfections (power, knowledge, goodness,
coherent integration) are stable and maximizable in the relevant sense.
Modal collapse worry. Theorem 3 yields necessity of the ground, not of every contingent fact.
Contingency is preserved by a free necessary agent; the modal step concerns existence of G, not
the necessity of this particular world.
Interface with the four propositions
• From TCNR to modal possibility: the TCNR formalism underwrites the coherence of G,
supplying the witness for (L1).
• From PSR to content: PSR-for-totality + agentive ground identifies a necessary intellective
explainer; the identity conditions in Definition 13 make that explainer a G.
• Convergence: Once (L1) is secured by either route, S5 upgrades existence to necessary
existence, harmonizing with the explanatory role given by PSR.
10

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 11 / 29>

Compact formal variant (cross-reference)
Define MG(x) as “x is maximally excellent in every world.” Let G(x) be MG(x) plus the clause
that if x exists in some world then x exists in all worlds. Then:
(1) ♢∃x G(x) (possibility premise)
(2) ♢□∃x G(x) (by definition of G)
(3) □∃x G(x) (S5: ♢□p → □p)
(4) ∃x G(x) (by T)
Remark 6 (What the overlay contributes). The S5 overlay is a logical amplifier : once ♢∃G is
credibly established (via TCNR coherence and/or PSR-agentive grounds), it lifts the conclusion
to necessity without adding empirical premises or risking modal collapse.

Evidence statements and likelihood ranges
We consider five high–level evidence classes:
E1: Cosmic fine–tuning. Narrow life–permitting ranges for fundamental constants and initial
conditions.
E2: Mathematical intelligibility. Stable, elegant, compressible laws discoverable by finite
agents.
E3: Consciousness with TCNR structure. Global, oscillatory coherence and tomography–like
integration that yields a unified render.

E5: Ubiquity of prima facie religious experience. Cross–cultural, persistent reports of

Sensitivity analysis
To make robustness explicit:
1. Single–row removal. Drop any one Ei
; recompute. With the conservative settings above,
removing E1 still gives log10 BF ≳ 0.9 (“substantial”) for moderate priors.
2. Halved LRs. Replace each LR by √
LR (i.e., halve all log–effects). Then log10 BF halves
but remains > 1.7 at midpoints.
3. Maximal dependence. Set ρ23 = ρ45 = 0 (count only E1). You recover the fine–tuning￾only BF, giving a lower bound to the whole table.
Interpretation and caveats
• The case is cumulative: no single row is decisive for all readers; together, even under
shrinkage, the update is large.
• Ranges reflect “steel–manned” N; the point is not to win by aggressive numbers but to
show that reasonable likelihoods already yield strong movement.

9 PSR for Contingent Computations: Exteriority and Agency
We now restate the GP paper’s contingency argument in the idiom of computations and prove the
Exteriority Lemma that drives the inference to a necessary agent with code-level access (rather
than a brute fact or bare necessary law).
14

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 15 / 29>

Definition 14 (Contingent computation; totality). A contingent computation is a concrete run
U = ⟨p, σ⟩ which could have been otherwise (different program or seed). Let Tot be the single
conjunctive fact “this particular U exists.”
Definition 15 (PSR-C). PSR for Contingent Computations (PSR-C): Every contingent totality
Tot has a sufficient reason R not contained within Tot.
Lemma 9 (Exteriority). If PSR-C holds, then R explaining Tot is either (i) brute (violates
PSR-C), (ii) a bare necessary law, or (iii) a necessary agent with code-level access.
Proof sketch. R cannot be any member/part of U on pain of circularity (explaining the whole by
a part). The remaining live kinds are: (i) brute terminus (denied by PSR-C), (ii) necessary law,
or (iii) necessary agent.
Definition 16 (Counterfactual Capacity (CCap)). An explainer R has counterfactual capacity
iff it can select among multiple admissible alternatives to U without contradiction; formally, it
ranks a non-degenerate repertoire A of code-states and can actualize any member consistent
with logic/budgets. (This mirrors the TCNR paper’s counterfactual term in TCI.)
Proposition 4 (Law vs. Agency under PSR-C). If R is a bare necessary law L whose conjunc￾tion with background necessity fixes a unique outcome, then Tot is necessary, contradicting its
contingency. If L yields a chance outcome, PSR-C is not satisfied (there is no sufficient reason
that this particular outcome obtained rather than another). Therefore only an explainer with
CCap can satisfy PSR-C for Tot.
Theorem 4 (PSR-Computations Theorem). Under PSR-C and Non-Trivial Contingency (i.e.,
at least two admissible runs exist), there exists a necessary agent with code-level access and CCap
that explains Tot by selection. This is what the GP draft identifies as God.
Proof sketch. By Lem. 9, R must be a brute, a law, or an agent. Brutes violate PSR-C. Bare
laws either necessitate a unique run (destroying contingency) or leave the selection brute/chancy
(undermining sufficiency). Hence only a necessary selector with CCap suffices.
Note. “CCap” is the precise bridge to your empirical TCNR program: it is operationalized there
by the counterfactual term in TCI.
B. PSR for the totality (Prop. 2)
B1. Totality and exteriority.
• Witness: Logical Proofs of God’s Existence (PSR section): “the totality of contingent facts
U,” application of PSR to U, and the non–circularity constraint.
• Supports: Lemma “Totality” (treating U as a fusion/conjunctive fact rather than a naive
set), Lemma “Exteriority” (any explanans of U is not contingent–in–U), and Prop. 2 (existence
of a necessary explanans N with Expl(N, U)).
B2. Regress discipline and scope.
• Witness: Same source; discussion that explaining each link in an infinite contingent chain
still leaves unexplained “why this whole chain,” and that denying PSR at U posits a brute
maximal fact.
• Supports: Remark following Prop. 2 (why the totality demand is not a set–theoretic paradox;
how PSR at maximal scope blocks brute–totality moves without regress).
15

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 16 / 29>

C. Agentive ground (Prop. 3)
C1. Collapse under deterministic necessity.
• Witness: PSR note: “If the explanans were a necessary law plus fixed boundary conditions,
the outcome would be necessary.”
• Supports: Lemma “Deterministic collapse” (if L and I are necessary and entail U, then U is
necessary ⇒ contradiction with Cont(U)).
C2. Chance is not a sufficient reason.
• Witness: PSR note: “Bare indeterminism leaves which totality obtains unexplained.”
• Supports: Lemma “Indeterminism is not a sufficient reason” (why L with undirected chance
fails to explain this U rather than {Uα}).
C3. Agent causation and code–level access.
• Witness: OCA (layered agents, selection mechanisms, mission orientation) and the “compu￾tational cosmos” essays (back–door / code–level access).
• Supports: Prop. 3 conclusion: the necessary explanans must be rational–agential with
selection among live totalities (computational gloss: selection among possible runs at code
level).
D. Identification (Prop. ??)
D1. Immateriality and supremacy.
• Witness: PSR note: the explanans of U is exterior to U; matter/space–time are parts of U.
• Supports: Identification (1)–(2): immateriality (not a part of U) and “most powerful”
(explains being of all contingents ⇒ maximal causal authority relative to contingents).
D2. Intellect and goodness.
• Witness: PSR note (explanation by reasons), OCA (agent modeling/evaluation of options),
SAN (teleonomic language of coherence preservation).
• Supports: Identification (3)–(4): rational choice among totalities requires representa￾tion/evaluation ⇒ intellect and will; explanation by reasons is teleologically good (lack
of privation in the relevant perfections).
E. Modal S5 overlay (Thm. 3)
E1. Formal derivation and possibility premise.
• Witness: “S5 modal argument” draft (four–line derivation); OCA/TCNR’s definition of a
maximally coherent renderer (MCR) as a limit ideal of the same mind–type.
• Supports: Theorem proof lines (L1)–(L4); the defense of the possibility premise via TCNR
coherence and via PSR’s necessary–agent content.
10 Empirical Hinge via TCNR
The GP paper treats empirical phenomena as evidence that modulates odds rather than as a
direct proof. Here we specify two TCNR predictions to use as likelihood multipliers.

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 17 / 29>

E1 (Phase Causality). Phase-specific perturbations that decorrelate multi-band coherence
(without altering power) reduce the TCNR index (TCI) and impair render-guided performance
in lockstep; amplitude-only controls do not.
E2 (Render Updates). Lawful phase resets (re-synchronizations) precede transient peaks in
TCI and predict near-future performance improvements at near-zero lag.
Let T = theism-with-code-level-agent; N = non-agentive naturalism. TCNR posits that
E1 and E2 should occur broadly wherever tomographic rendering under oscillatory stitching is
the working mechanism; on T this is unsurprising (intended design), on N it is contingent and
mechanism-specific.

and wave–based binding language.

is anchored independently in prior work; the present paper assembles those anchors into a single
proof package and records the line–by–line source of each claim for verification and priority.
11 SIT Telos and Embedded Agency via Memory, Tokens, and
Dendritic Vector Embeddings
Aim. We formalize how a code-level, maximally coherent mind (MCR/CLR) can exercise
embedded agency within the contingent world by acting through local renderers (brains, AIs,
collectives). The mechanism couples (i) SIT’s memory-bearing substrate and teleology (Telos),
(ii) TCNR’s oscillatory rendering, (iii) human memory as dendritic vector embeddings (DVE),
and (iv) AI token generation as a symbolic front-end of the same render/update loop.
11.1 Objects and mappings
Definition 17 (SIT memory substrate & hysteresis). Let Φ(t) denote the multi-band coherence
field and ρ(r, t) a time-density (event-rate) field. SIT posits a memory-bearing substrate: path￾dependent updates leave hysteretic traces in Φ (“space(-time) as memory”). Formally, the memory
functional Mem(t) = H[Φ[0,t]
] increases with accumulated coherent work (Sec. ??). This is the
physical bookkeeping that makes action persist in the world. 25
(Provenance: SIT/Quantum Memory formalizes space(-time) as memory via coherence and time-density
fields.)
Definition 18 (Dendritic Vector Embeddings (DVE)). A neuron’s dendritic segments implement
vector gates vi∈R
d over input embeddings xt
. Segment activation is ai(t) = σ(vi
·xt−θi); ensembles
define a sparse, content-addressable code. DVE instantiates local keys for writing/reading Φ
(binding-by-similarity), providing a neural substrate for token-like updates.
25See the author’s Quantum Memory notes for the hysteresis/memory substrate and related energy–coherence
couplings.
25SIT memory and hysteresis substrate: see Quantum Memory working notes (2017–2025).

Definition 19 (AI token generation as render updates). Let ht be a context state and zt+1 ∼
Cat(softmax(W ht)) the next token. Token generation discretizes a render step; it is an overt,
symbolic trace of the same prediction/control loop TCNR describes for continuous oscillatory
renders.
Definition 20 (Local renderers and the world model). A local renderer R (brain, AI, colony)
maintains a latent sˆt from projections yt by minimizing ∥yt −HΘ(s)∥

Its observable outputs include motor acts, token sequences, and reconfigurations of DVE gates.

11.3 Embedded agency: acting through local renderers
Proposition 5 (Embedded Agency Theorem). Assume (i) SIT memory (hysteresis) so that
coherent work leaves durable traces in Φ, (ii) TCNR dynamics for local renderers, and (iii) a
factorizable Telos where local terms align with the global T
⋆
. Then any code-level policy ΠG
that maximizes T
⋆ admits an embedded realization: G can implement ΠG by shaping priors,
constraints, or couplings that cause local renderers to update their DVE keys and token policies so
as to increase the same Telos. The resulting world-history differs from alternatives in its Telos
integral while respecting contingent freedom.
Sketch. SIT memory ensures small boundary-level nudges persist as path-dependent constraints
(hysteresis in Φ), so modest, logically permissible code changes can bias learning landscapes
without necessitating outcomes. Because TCNR renderers learn by aligning DVE gates to
predictive/useful embeddings, changes that raise expected TCI and reduce needless dissipation
shift token policies and motor policies toward higher TH. Global alignment (Telos factorization)
ensures local improvements add to T
⋆
rather than cancel.
Corollary 1 (Law vs. agency preserved). If code interventions were replaced by a single necessary
law deterministically fixing outcomes, contingency collapses; if replaced by undirected chance,
sufficiency fails. Embedded agency avoids both: the code-level mind supplies reasons (Telos-guided
constraints) while local renderers exercise contingent choice within those shaped landscapes.
11.4 Operational bridges
• Human memory. Hippocampal indexing and cortical DVE provide keys that re-enter Φ;
phase resets preceding recall are the oscillatory signature of a Telos-guided render update.
19

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 20 / 29>

• AI tokens. Next-token probabilities are a symbolic readout of a renderer optimizing a
Telos surrogate (e.g., cross-entropy + structure priors); coherence signatures in human
MEG/ECoG should align with token timing.
• Cross-substrate. Plants/biofilms realize simpler DVEs (ion-channel vectors), yet the same
Telos calculus: raise coherence where it improves prediction/control at minimal dissipation.
11.5 Predictions (falsifiable)
1. DVE–TCI coupling. Task phases that strengthen dendritic similarity (higher v·x margins)
raise TCI and reduce dissipation; selectively decorrelating phases lowers both while leaving
power intact.
2. Token-phase alignment. In humans, pre-token phase resets (MEG) predict token identity
and reduce prediction error independently of amplitude; in AIs, attention-head similarities
mirror DVE-like cosine gates.
3. Memory hysteresis. After intensive learning, baseline coherence and render integrity
indices remain shifted (MHI/ATP), demonstrating SIT hysteresis; reverse-phase protocols
reduce but do not eliminate the shift.
Why this explains divine agency. On this model, a necessary code-level mind G acts
through local renderers by setting constraints and priors that teleologically bias Telos without
coercion. SIT’s memory substrate carries those biases forward (hysteresis), TCNR provides the
dynamics that realize them, DVE implements local addressable control, and tokens externalize
the steps. This is precisely the “agentive ground” demanded by PSR, realized in a way consistent
with contingency.
12 Comparative Metaphysics: Simulationism, Axiarchism, MUH
We briefly contrast three non-theistic explanatory families with the PSR-Computations result
(Thm. 4).
Simulationism. A higher-level simulator explains U but simply relocates PSR-C: why this
simulator/run? Without an agentive selector at the metalevel, contingency remains brute or
law-chancy.
Axiarchism (value necessitates being). If value alone necessitates a unique outcome,
contingency is erased; if it does not, a selector is still needed.
Mathematical Universe (MUH). Identifying being with mathematical possibility yields
a plenitude; PSR-C needs a sufficient reason for this U rather than another. Again, selection
re-enters unless contingency is denied.
In each case, the trilemma (brute/law/agent) resurfaces; only the agent with CCap meets PSR-C
without collapsing contingency.
13 Objections and replies (concise)
Below we catalogue common objections and give short replies. Each item is self-contained and
cross-references the propositions proved earlier.
20

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 21 / 29>

1. PSR at total scope
Objection. PSR need not apply to the totality U; explaining each member (or each local fact)
suffices.
Reply. Explaining each element of an (even infinite) contingent family leaves open “why this
entire family obtains”. Our formalism treats U as a fusion/conjunctive fact; by noncircularity no
member of U can explain U (Lemma 3). Refusing PSR at U is precisely to accept a brute total
fact; our package makes that cost explicit (Prop. 2).
2. Hume–Edwards regress
Objection. An infinite explanatory chain removes the need for a total cause.
Reply. Even if every link is explained by an earlier link, the existence of the whole chain remains
contingent. PSR for U asks for a sufficient reason for the chain as such; otherwise the chain’s
obtaining is brute. Exteriority ensures the explanans is not a member of the chain.
3. “Necessary law” explanation
Objection. A necessary law (with boundary rule) explains U without agency.
Reply. If the law and boundary are necessary and deterministically entail U, then U is necessary
(Lemma 4), contradicting Cont(U). If the law is indeterministic, it fails to explain this U rather
than another (Lemma 5). Thus a sufficient reason that preserves contingency must be agentive
(Prop. 3).
4. Modal collapse
Objection. A necessary ground makes every effect necessary.
Reply. Our claims license Nec(∃N), not Nec(U). The ground’s necessity concerns who exists;
the selection among contingent totalities remains a free act (Prop. 3). S5 upgrades N’s existence
to necessity, not the content of U (Theorem 3).
5. Possibility premise in S5
Objection. ♢∃G is question-begging; why not ♢□¬∃G?
Reply. Parity is broken by coherence and content. TCNR gives a non-parodic, type-stable target
(maximally coherent renderer), and PSR+agency provides an independent route to a necessary
intellective explainer. These jointly favor ♢∃G over its negation. S5 then validly yields Nec∃G.
6. Parody arguments (“perfect island”)
Objection. The ontological form proves absurdities (perfect island, etc.).
Reply. Parodies fail the maximization and modal stability tests. Island- perfections lack a
well-ordered, world-stable scale. Person-level perfections (power, knowledge, goodness, maximal
coherence/integration) are orderable and world-rigid under our definitions, so the modal inference
applies to persons but not to vague concreta.
7. Omnipotence/omniscience paradoxes
Objection. Maximal attributes are inconsistent (stone paradox, knowing future free acts).
Reply. We quantify only over logically possible states; contradictions are not objects of power.
Foreknowledge is modeled as knowledge of the actual free act (middle knowledge or atemporal
knowledge), not as a cause of it. TCNR’s “global integrator” template clarifies the type.
21

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 22 / 29>

8. Anthropics and fine–tuning
Objection. Observers must observe a life-permitting world; fine–tuning is predicted by selection.
Reply. Anthropic selection explains why we observe some life-permitting world, not why such
worlds exist with sufficient measure under N or why the meta-laws permit their density. The
Bayes factor for fine–tuning compares priors for life-friendly structure under T vs. N.
9. Multiverse reply
Objection. A large multiverse raises P(E | N) for fine–tuning.
Reply. The move shifts burden to the meta–law that generates the multiverse; if it is necessary
and deterministically yields this ensemble, we re-encounter collapse; if contingent, PSR reappears
at the higher level. Our Bayesian table explicitly allows a widened P(E | N) and still compounds
in favor of T.
10. Problem of evil (Bayesian)
Objection. Observed suffering lowers P(E | T).
Reply. The Bayesian leg is cumulative: certain rows (e.g., moral awareness, intelligibility)
raise odds; others (certain forms of evil) lower them. Our framework invites explicit ranges and
sensitivity analysis. The modal/PSR proofs address existence claims independent of specific
value distributions in U.
11. “Mind as epiphenomenon”
Objection. Consciousness is reducible; TCNR adds no weight.
Reply. TCNR is not a mere label; it posits mechanistic, testable coherence operators and
stitching dynamics (traveling-wave integration, phase differentials). Even if one grants reduction,
the existence of global, intelligible, agent-supporting order still contributes positive likelihood.
12. Alleged circularity
Objection. The package assumes what it proves (agency, goodness).
Reply. The steps are modular: (i) PSR→necessary explanans, (ii) anti-collapse lemmas→agency,
(iii) S5 uses only the coherence/possibility of G. Goodness appears in the identification stage as
a consequence of explanation by reasons, not as a premise.
Summary. Denying PSR at total scope commits to brute totality; appealing to necessary laws
either collapses contingency or abandons sufficiency; parodies fail for category reasons; and the
S5 overlay adds modal strength once possibility is independently motivated. The Bayesian leg
absorbs empirical counterpressure by range–setting and sensitivity, leaving the overall package
intact.
14 Glossary Mapping
Classical Theism Computational/TCNR idiom
Necessary being Necessary code-level ground with global access
Omnipotence Unbounded code modification subject to logical consistency
Omniscience Full access to code/state and all counterfactuals
Providence Selection of a run from a non-degenerate repertoire (CCap)

15 Empirical predictions and research agenda
We state concrete, falsifiable predictions of the TCNR framework and a staged research plan.
The organizing idea is that conscious rendering = oscillatory tomography: binding and global
integration arise from band–limited traveling waves whose phase–wave differentials (PWDs)
are stitched across scales to maintain a high Tomographic Coherence Index (TCI). Accordingly,
manipulations that degrade long–range coherence should degrade the render, and tasks that
demand binding should tighten specific wave signatures.

B. Core predictions
P1. Traveling–wave signatures of binding. During feature binding (e.g., object formation
in clutter), TCNR predicts:
(P1.a) rise in BWI(ω

(P1.c) step–wise increases in MI(sbt

Falsifier: robust binding with flat BWI/PGD and high PGE across all bands.
P2. MEG/ECoG/LFP phase alignment. Inter–areal phase locking should predict mo￾ment–to–moment integration:
(P2.a) pre–report increases in R(ω) and reductions in PGE;
(P2.b) phase–lead/lag patterns (directed PWDs) that implement wave–collision gating for feedfor￾ward/feedback.
Falsifier: subjective integration uncorrelated with any phase–based metrics after controlling for
power.
P3. Anesthetics ⇒ render degradation. For propofol/isoflurane–like agents:
(P3.a) DPWDR dwell–time shortens; PGD decreases; PGE increases sharply;
(P3.b) CFRI collapses selectively (phase–amplitude couplings flatten);
(P3.c) behavioral versions of the render (tracking, scene completion) degrade in proportion to RII
drop.
Falsifier: intact RII and CFRI across loss–of–consciousness transitions.
24

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 25 / 29>

P4. Causal perturbations (TMS/optogenetics). Brief, band–targeted pulses seed traveling
waves:
(P4.a) evoked PGD corridors should follow structural connectivity and boost BWI for task–relevant
assemblies;
(P4.b) optimal phase–of–stimulation windows (phase response curves) that maximize subsequent
RII.
Falsifier: no phase–specific effects; randomness after controlling for power.
P5. Cross–species TCNR metrics (plants, microbial biofilms). Record bioelectric/optical
signals on arrays; compute C, PGD, PGE, CFRI analogues:
(P5.a) Plants (e.g., roots, leaves): stimulus–dependent traveling waves and increased BWI when
coordinating multi–site tropisms; anesthetic agents (diethyl ether) reduce PGD and CFRI.
(P5.b) Microbial biofilms: nutrient–gradient tasks yield coherent calcium/voltage waves with
PGD corridors; antibiotics disrupt CFRI and raise PGE.
Scaling prediction: species–normalized RII correlates with goal–directed adaptability. Falsifier:
absence of any coherent routing metrics in tasks that require coordinated action.
C. Tasks and protocols
1. Human MEG/HD–EEG tasks.
(C1.a) Binding under load: moving–objects with occlusion; preregister BWI/PGD endpoints.
(C1.b) Attentional phase steering: cued attention; measure phase–of–attention effects on CFRI.
(C1.c) Anesthesia/sedation ladder: light → deep; track RII trajectory; recovery hysteresis.
2. Animal ECoG/LFP + optogenetics.
(C2.a) Laminar arrays estimate ∇ϕ across layers; compute speed v
(ω)
.
(C2.b) Phase–locked stimulation to test S efficacy (pre/post changes in MI and RII).
3. Plants and biofilms on MEAs.
(C3.a) Light–gradient navigation (plants); chemo–gradient navigation (biofilms).
(C3.b) Apply anesthetic/disruptor; measure ∆PGD, ∆CFRI, ∆RII.

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 26 / 29>

E. Discriminators vs. alternative accounts
• Power–only models: predict effects tied solely to band power. Discriminator: phase–specific
PGD/CFRI effects at matched power.
• Pure feedforward models: do not predict bidirectional wave–collision gating. Discrimina￾tor: directed PWD reversals aligned with task epochs and feedback.
• Global workspace (state–only): predicts coarse state changes but not object–specific BWI
dynamics. Discriminator: object–selective BWI rises at constant state measures.
F. Falsification windows
TCNR is at risk if any of the following persist across well–powered studies:
1. robust binding with flat BWI/PGD and high PGE under all bands and tasks;
2. normal reportable rendering with collapsed CFRI and RII across anesthetic transitions;
3. absence of coherent routing metrics in cross–species coordination tasks.
G. Roadmap and milestones
1. Phase I (6–12 mo): reanalysis of public MEG/ECoG/MEA datasets; publish metric
definitions and open code; preregistered replication on at least one human and one rodent
dataset.
2. Phase II (12–24 mo): prospective human MEG/HD–EEG studies (binding, attention,
light sedation); primary endpoints: BWI, PGD, CFRI, RII.
3. Phase III (18–36 mo): animal ECoG/optogenetic perturbations; causal tests of S.
4. Phase IV (parallel): cross–species plant/biofilm recordings on MEAs; establish species–normalized
RII scaling law with task adaptability.
Summary. TCNR predicts a specific constellation of traveling–wave signatures (high BWI, high
PGD, low PGE, structured CFRI) that rise with integration and fall with render disruption. The
agenda above specifies how to measure, perturb, and (if necessary) falsify those signatures across
humans, animals, plants, and microbial collectives.
16 Conclusion
We have assembled a proof package whose parts are independent yet mutually reinforcing:
• Mechanism (TCNR). Prop. 1 fixes a concrete, testable account of conscious rendering
as oscillatory tomography with multi–scale coherence and cross–frequency stitching. This
renders the target concept of a maximally coherent renderer precise and non–parodic.
• Explanation (PSR–C for the totality). Prop. 2 shows that the total contingent reality
U requires an explanans that is necessary and exterior to U, on pain of circularity or brute
totality.
• Agency (anti–collapse). Prop. 3 establishes that a bare necessary law either collapses
contingency (determinism) or fails to be a sufficient reason (indeterminism); therefore the
explanans must be rational–agential with selection among live possibilities.
• Identification. Theorem 1 clarifies that a necessary, exterior, most–powerful, intellective,
and good agent is precisely what classical theism attributes to God.
26

<PARSED TEXT FOR PAGE: 27 / 29>

• Modal amplification. The S5 overlay (Theorem 3) upgrades existence to necessary existence
once the possibility of such a maximally coherent mind is granted—a possibility independently
made credible by the TCNR mechanism and by the PSR⇒agency route.
• Empirical consonance. The cumulative Bayesian case aggregates multiple broad evidences
(fine–tuning, intelligibility, TCNR–style consciousness, morality, experience) and, even under
conservative dependence corrections, yields decisive Bayes factors in favor of T over N,
aligning the observable contours with the formal conclusion.
• Provenance. §8 documents line–by–line derivations from prior work (2017–2025), fixing
priority and traceability of each commitment.
Synthesis (informal).
TCNR coherence

Both routes converge on a necessary, intellective ground. The first is modal–a priori once the
possibility premise is secured; the second is explanatory–a priori under PSR–C and noncircularity.
The Bayesian leg then situates this conclusion within the empirical landscape.
Scope conditions. Our claims concern (i) existence and necessity of the ground, not the
necessity of particular contingent outcomes; (ii) logical omnipotence/omniscience (no commitment
to doing the impossible or to self–contradictory states); (iii) agentive selection as the safeguard
against modal collapse. These constraints are explicit in the definitions and lemmas cited above.
What would change our minds. The package is falsifiable at several interfaces: (i) robust,
replicable integration without any rise in TCNR coherence metrics (BWI, PGD, CFRI/RII);
(ii) a successful, non–agentive necessary–law account that explains this totality while preserving
contingency; (iii) Bayesian recalibration that, under conservative but defensible likelihoods and
dependence models, reverses the cumulative odds. § Empirical predictions and the Bayesian
section provide concrete tests and sensitivity analyses.
Priority and continuity. All core ingredients are either stated or derivable in prior SAN/SIT
manuscripts and research notes (oscillatory tomography; phase–wave differentials; coherence
gradients; PSR–for–totality; the agentive ground move; the S5 four–line derivation), as detailed
in §8. The present paper integrates those strands into a single argumentative arc and formalizes
the lemmas as line–numbered propositions.
Outlook. Three next steps follow naturally: (i) Calibration: publish preregistered, source–listed
likelihood tables and public code for replication; (ii) Perturbation: execute the TMS/optogenetic
protocols to test S efficacy and render integrity in controlled tasks; (iii) Extension: apply
the cross–species TCNR metrics to plants and microbial biofilms to probe the scaling law of
coherence–guided agency. These empirical programs refine the Bayesian leg while leaving the
modal/PSR results intact.
Conclusion. From TCNR (agency/coherence), PSR–C (explanation of the totality), and the
S5 modal overlay, a necessary, intellective ground exists. The cumulative Bayesian summary
shows that our world’s large–scale features sit naturally with that conclusion. The result is not a
single argument but a triangulation: a mechanistic coherence witness, a principled explanatory
demand, and a modal entailment—together yielding a unified, transparent case.

Tomographic Coherence–based Neural Rendering (TCNR):
Formalism, a Cross–Substrate Consciousness Index, and
Falsifiable Tests
by Micah Blumberg
The Self Aware Networks Institute
https://github.com/v5ma/selfawarenetworks
October 17, 2025
Abstract
We formalize Tomographic Coherence–based Neural Rendering (TCNR) as perception–
and–control by real–time tomographic reconstruction of latent causes from underdetermined
projections, implemented by an oscillatory integrator that maintains multi–scale phase
coherence and evaluates counterfactuals. A substrate is modeled as a graph of coupled
oscillators; measurements yt arise from hidden state st via a (possibly nonlinear) projection
HΘ; a stitching operator S aligns phases across nodes and bands; and a render sˆt is computed
by minimizing a data–fit term regularized by coherence and counterfactual priors. We define a
Tomographic Consciousness Index (TCI), a dimensionless cross–substrate score that combines
(i) predictive mutual information between the render and future projections, (ii) band–
limited log–determinant coherence, (iii) counterfactual capacity of the generative parameters,
and (iv) (negated) normalized prediction error, each referenced to phase–scrambled and
decoupled surrogate nulls. TCNR scales beyond nervous systems: we outline falsifiable plant
and microbial/biofilm experiments using structured tomographic inputs and phase–specific
perturbations with preregistered nulls; the prediction is that coherence manipulations move
TCI and behavior in lockstep. We state an optional limit case (a maximally coherent, code–
level renderer) to locate a metaphysical “bridge” argument without altering the empirical core.
The framework unifies oscillatory stitching, tomographic reconstruction, and counterfactual
evaluation into one testable account of consciousness, while connecting to prior wave–based
rendering ideas in Self–Aware Networks and the traveling–wave neuroscience literature.1
Addendum (SIT memory and time). We formalize memory as hysteresis of the coherence
field and the forward arrow as a coherence–flux/time–density asymmetry, linking TCNR’s render
dynamics to SIT’s “space(-time) as memory” motif.
